                                "is_missing": false,
                                "text": "Zoho CRM\u2019s free plan supports up to 3 users and provides core CRM functionality including leads, contacts, accounts and basic pipeline management. Zoho also offers nonprofit discounts on paid plans for registered charities.\n\nNonprofits searching for a free CRM want to know one thing: is it actually useful, or is the free plan a stripped-down trial that forces an upgrade within weeks? Zoho CRM\u2019s free plan is genuinely more capable than most free-tier CRM offerings \u2014 but it has real limitations that affect whether it can serve as a long-term solution for a growing organisation. This guide covers what the free plan includes, where it falls short and when the paid plans (which come with a nonprofit discount) become worth the investment. For the broader nonprofit CRM context, see the Zoho CRM for nonprofits hub. \n\nGet Help Choosing the Right Zoho CRM Plan for Your Nonprofit \u00b7 The absence of workflow rules on the free plan means no automated gift acknowledgements, no lapsed donor re-engagement triggers, no grant deadline reminders and no volunteer coordination workflows.\n\nThe Zoho CRM free plan is genuinely appropriate for a nonprofit in one of these situations: Starting from nothing: a newly established organisation that needs a donor database and basic relationship tracking before it has the budget for a paid tool. The free plan provides a clean foundation that can be upgraded when resources allow. Very small operation: a charity run by three or fewer staff or trustees with under 100 donors and no volunteer programme. The manual workflows are manageable at this scale and the cost of any paid tool may not be justified. Evaluating CRM before committing: organisations that want to understand how Zoho CRM works before committing to a paid plan. \n\nThe free plan provides a genuine sense of the interface and data model.                                 "type": "knowledge",
                                "title": "Zoho for Nonprofits: A Guide to CRM, Donors and Funds | Aaxonix",
                                "url": "https://aaxonix.com/resources/zoho-for-nonprofits/",
                                "metadata": {
                                    "type": "webpage_metadata",
                                    "site_domain": "aaxonix.com",
                                    "favicon_url": "https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?sz=64&domain=aaxonix.com",

                                },
                                "is_missing": false,
                                "text": "Can one Zoho CRM handle donors, volunteers and members together? Yes, but only after it is remodelled. Out of the box, Zoho CRM is built around leads and deals for sales. A nonprofit needs separate donor, volunteer and member records, engagement ...\n\nNo. Zoho does not sell a purpose built fundraising or grants module. Donor pipelines, moves management and grant reporting are built on Zoho CRM, Zoho Creator and Zoho Books, which takes configuration work but gives a nonprofit more flexibility than a fixed fundraising platform. ... Zoho generally offers discounted or free access to parts of its suite for eligible registered nonprofits, so total cost is usually lower than a commercial rollout. Eligibility rules and the depth of the discount vary by country and by app, so confirm current terms with Zoho before budgeting. Can one Zoho CRM handle donors, volunteers and members together? Yes, but only after it is remodelled. Out of the box, Zoho CRM is built around leads and deals for sales. \n\nBook a free consultation \u00b7 None of this requires buying every app in Zoho One on day one. Most nonprofits get further by remodelling CRM and Forms properly first, then adding Books, Creator, Campaigns and Analytics as programs and reporting needs actually grow.\n\nZoho CRM, like most CRMs, ships with Leads, Deals, Accounts and Contacts, organised around a sales funnel: a lead gets qualified, a deal moves through stages, and it closes won or lost. A nonprofit that installs Zoho CRM and enters data on top of those defaults tends to hit the same problem within a few months. A donation gets logged as a closed won deal, a volunteer application gets logged as a lead, and reporting on donor retention, recurring gifts or household relationships becomes close to impossible, because none of those concepts exist in a sales pipeline. The fix is to decide the model before entering data, not after.",

                                "is_missing": false,
                                "text": "In practice, nonprofits should contact Zoho sales or a Zoho partner for exact rates (often 10\u201330% off). Zoho also supports educational licenses of certain apps; details are available on Zoho\u2019s websites for Education.\n\nZoho Desk also offers non-profit and educational discounts on request, though specific rates aren\u2019t published. (Like Zoho Mail, NGOs are advised to contact Zoho sales for details.) Note that Desk prices are region-dependent; the INR prices above apply to India, whereas US prices would be roughly $6, $10, $18, $30 for Express\u2013Enterprise. Zoho Analytics (business intelligence) has four subscription tiers and a free tier: Free Plan: $0, supports 2 users, 5 workspaces, and up to 10,000 rows[34]. Good for lightweight reporting needs. Basic: $25/month, 2 users, 0.5 million rows of data, unlimited dashboards[35]. Standard: $50/month, 5 users, 1 million rows. Premium: $125/month, 15 users, 5 million rows. Enterprise: $495/month, 50 users, 50 million rows. \n\n(The above pricing is monthly; annual plans save ~20%[36].) All plans allow unlimited reports/dashboards.\n\n(All prices exclude VAT/GST as noted by Zoho[4].) Note that Zoho One includes classic support by default; companies needing faster or more comprehensive support can add Premium (20% extra of subscription) or Enterprise support (25% extra)[5]. Zoho offers no further bundle or nonprofit discount on One (Zoho states pricing is already competitive)[6]. Zoho CRM has a Free edition (up to 3 users) and four paid tiers. Annual (\u201cbilled yearly\u201d) and monthly pricing differ. According to Zoho and third-party sources[7][8], the paid editions (per user per month) are approximately: ... Each plan builds on the previous. For example, Standard adds core CRM (sales automation, basic reports), while Professional adds inventory modules and advanced features, Enterprise adds multi-user-per-license sharing and configurable modules, and Ultimate includes AI/analytics add-ons.",

                        "flags": null,
                        "type": "text",
                        "text": "Good news: a fair amount is available to you *right now*, even before 12A/80G/FCRA come through \u2014 and a couple of things are worth doing this week because they unlock everything else later. Here's the full picture, organized by what's usable at each stage.\n\n## The one thing to do this week: NGO Darpan registration\n\nThis is separate from 12A/80G/FCRA, is free, and needs only your trust registration certificate, PAN, and basic details (trustee Aadhaar, bank passbook). As soon as your trust is registered and has basic documents like PAN and address proof, you can apply, and registration typically takes about 15\u201330 working days for verification. Do this immediately because it's a mandatory prerequisite for FCRA registration, and the Darpan Unique ID must be entered in your FCRA application later. It also makes you eligible to apply for government grants, schemes, and CSR funding down the line.\n\n## What you can use *right now*, with just the trust deed + PAN (no 12A/80G/FCRA needed)\n\n**Donations infrastructure:**\n- **Razorpay / Cashfree / PhonePe Business** \u2014 you can start accepting donations today. Razorpay Payment Pages let you accept donations online without even needing a website, using just your organization name, address proof, and PAN copy for KYC. The catch: without 80G, you can issue a plain receipt, but not one that gives the *donor* a tax deduction \u2014 that comes automatically once 80G is granted (Razorpay can auto-generate 80G receipts once you have it).\n- **Canva for Nonprofits** \u2014 100% free for registered nonprofits, NGOs, and social impact organizations with up to 50 users, and eligibility is based on being registered as a nonprofit/charitable organization in your country with a mission-driven, non-commercial purpose \u2014 a registered trust deed generally qualifies, doesn't strictly require 12A/80G.\n- **Zoho CRM free tier** \u2014 supports up to 3 users with core CRM functionality \u2014 leads, contacts, accounts, basic pipeline \u2014 usable immediately for donor/volunteer tracking, no registration status required. (Zoho's *discounted* nonprofit tier does ask for 80G/FCRA later.)\n- **TechSoup validation** \u2014 worth starting now. It doesn't require 12A/80G itself, just proof of registration such as your governing documents or a government publication evidencing registration status \u2014 this validation is the gateway to Microsoft/Adobe/other discounts later.\n\n## What's gated behind 12A/80G (your next milestone, 1\u20132 months out)\n\n- **Google Ad Grants** ($10,000/month in free Search ads) \u2014 India eligibility explicitly requires a valid 12A/80G certificate, plus an active website with substantial content.\n- **Microsoft 365 for Nonprofits** (free Business Basic + discounted Standard/Premium) \u2014 Indian nonprofits are verified via TechSoup using 80G, 12A, and trust deed documentation.\n- **Zoho's actual nonprofit discount** (vs. the free CRM tier) \u2014 requires valid 80G and FCRA registration for Indian trusts/societies/Section 8 companies.\n\n## What's gated behind FCRA specifically (the longer-term milestone)\n\nThis is the one to plan for, because it's stricter than most people expect: **Google for Nonprofits** (the umbrella program covering free Workspace, Ad Grants access, YouTube Nonprofit Program, Maps credits) has a peculiar India-specific rule \u2014 Indian NGOs must be registered under 12A/80G AND hold FCRA registration, confirmed directly on Google's own eligibility page: all eligible organizations in India must be registered under the Foreign Contribution Regulations Act. This is unusual \u2014 most countries only need charitable registration \u2014 but it's real, and there are ongoing community threads of young Indian NGOs asking Google for exceptions, generally without success. So: budget Google Workspace as a paid (or discounted-via-TechSoup) tool for now, not free, until FCRA comes through.\n\nNote FCRA itself typically requires ~3 years of operational existence in India before you're eligible to apply (it's not just paperwork \u2014 it's a waiting period), so this is realistically a later-stage goal, not a 1\u20132 month one like 12A/80G.\n\n## Meta/Facebook \u2014 a caveat worth knowing\n\nMeta's on-platform donate button and native fundraising tools are only available in the US, UK, Canada and Australia \u2014 in other countries, including India, Meta only lets you link out to a donation page on your own website. So plan for Instagram/Facebook as *traffic drivers to your own Razorpay-powered donation page*, not as a payment channel themselves.\n\n## Mapping this to your 8 requirements\n\n| Requirement | Usable now | Needs 12A/80G | Needs FCRA |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1. Website | Domain done \u2713; build on WordPress/low-cost hosting | Google Ad Grants for traffic | Google for Nonprofits full suite |\n| 2. Digital marketing | Canva (free), organic social, Meta linking out | Google Ad Grants ($10K/mo) | \u2014 |\n| 3. Trust management | Zoho CRM free tier, Google Sheets/Notion | Zoho nonprofit discount | Google Workspace free |\n| 4. Donations | Razorpay/Cashfree (no 80G receipt yet) | 80G-auto-receipts | Foreign donations legally require FCRA regardless |\n| 5. Volunteers | Zoho CRM/Forms, NGO Darpan listing | \u2014 | \u2014 |\n| 6. Helping the needy | Independent of registration status | \u2014 | \u2014 |\n| 7. Legal docs/receipts | Manual PAN-based receipts | 80G tax-deduction receipts | FCRA utilization reports |\n| 8. CMS | Zoho suite, WordPress, free tiers | Microsoft 365 discount | Google Workspace free |\n\nA quick note since your domain and trust are both fresh: keep 12A/80G and NGO Darpan moving in parallel rather than sequentially \u2014 Darpan has no prerequisites and unlocks credibility/CSR eligibility faster, while 12A/80G unlocks the donor-facing tax benefits and Ad Grants.\n\nWant me to go deeper into any one piece \u2014 like a concrete website tech-stack recommendation, or a step-by-step for the Razorpay/Cashfree donation setup?",

This is broad enough that I should probably suggest doing focused searches across these different topics.\n```\nThis block is not supported on your current device yet.\n```\n\nNow I'm confirming that Google Ad Grants requires 501(c)(3) status, so they'll need to work toward that first, but I should search for other immediate options like Meta's nonprofit program for India, Microsoft's offerings, TechSoup India, Canva's nonprofit access, Darpan benefits, and available CMS platforms.\n```\nThis block is not supported on your current device yet.\n```\n\nMeta's native fundraising tools aren't available in India, but nonprofits there can still use the donate button feature to link to external donation platforms, along with Meta Business Suite ads. Now I need to check what Microsoft, TechSoup India, and Canva offer for nonprofits in that region.\n```\nThis block is not supported on your current device yet.\n```\n\nNow I'm looking at what NGOs can actually do right away without 12A/80G status\u2014things like setting up a website through free hosting platforms, using domain registrars, leveraging free design tools, accessing GitHub's nonprofit program, Zoho's NGO offerings, and Cloudflare or Netlify for hosting. I should also explore donation platforms available in India that don't require 80G certification, like Milaap, Ketto, ImpactGuru, and DonationBox, plus what they can accomplish during the pre-12A/80G phase.\n```\nThis block is not supported on your current device yet.\n```\n\nNow I'm looking into NGO Darpan benefits and CSR registration requirements, then exploring donation management platforms suitable for small Indian NGOs\u2014checking options like Give.do and payment solutions that work without needing 80G certification, including crowdfunding platforms and payment gateways that support nonprofits.\n```\nThis block is not supported on your current device yet.\n```\n\nNow I'm looking at domain-specific tools for nonprofits\u2014hosting options, GitHub, Cloudflare's nonprofit program, Zoho, and donation plugins like GiveWP. I'm also checking payment gateways that work for donations without 80G certification, like Razorpay and Instamojo, which only require PAN and bank account details, though I should note that donations won't be tax-deductible for donors without that certification. I have enough information to put together a thorough, well-organized response, and I'm just verifying a few more details on Zoho.\n\nLet me do a couple more targeted searches\u2014one for Canva, GitHub, and Zoho nonprofit programs, and another for donation payment gateways in India that don't require 80G status.\n```\nThis block is not supported on your current device yet.\n```\n\nNow I'm organizing this into a clear roadmap\u2014first checking what's immediately actionable versus what depends on getting their 12A/80G and FCRA approvals, then mapping out each of their eight requirements against available programs, and finally laying out a recommended sequence for them to follow. Compiling the full list of source documents for citations across compliance, fundraising platforms, and tech assistance programs... I need to be careful about citing sources with personal contact information, so I'll rely on the general documents instead for the Razorpay and payment gateway requirements rather than the presentation that contains PII. Now I'm ready to structure the response with clear headers and minimal citations, keeping things concise and well-organized.Good news first: even with 12A/80G/FCRA still pending, there's a lot you can set up *right now* that won't need to be redone later. Here's the landscape, mapped to your 8 requirements.\n\n## The core gating factor to understand\n\nAlmost every \"free tech/marketing for nonprofits\" program in India gates on **12A + 80G** (sometimes both, sometimes just one), verified through **TechSoup India**:\n\n- **Google Ad Grants** \u2014 up to $10,000/month in free Search ad credit \u2014 requires a registered charitable organization with a valid 12A/80G certificate and an active website with substantial content. Applied for via registering as an NGO member of NASSCOM Foundation's program, which acts as TechSoup's local partner in India for Google's verification.\n- **Google Workspace for Nonprofits** \u2014 available for NGOs registered under 12A and 80G, applied through TechSoup India, with 2\u20134 week processing.\n- **Microsoft 365 for Nonprofits** \u2014 Business Basic is free for up to 300 users for verified Indian nonprofits with 80G/12A status via Microsoft Philanthropies through TechSoup India, and Business Standard/Premium come at a steep discount.\n- **Canva for Nonprofits** \u2014 100% free Canva Pro access for registered NGOs, again gated behind TechSoup verification.\n- **TechSoup catalog broadly** (Adobe, Zoom, Slack for Charities, security tools) \u2014 same story: organizations must first be validated by TechSoup as operating on a nonprofit basis for the public benefit, then meet each vendor's individual criteria.\n\n**So the single highest-leverage move right now is pushing 12A/80G through fast** \u2014 everything downstream (Google, Microsoft, Canva, Ad Grants) unlocks off the back of that one certificate pair.\n\n## What you can do *this week*, with zero gating\n\n**1. NGO Darpan registration (do this immediately, it's free and has no minimum-age requirement)**\nA newly formed NGO can register on Darpan right away \u2014 there's no minimum age requirement, though applying for specific government schemes may require the org to be at least a year old. It's foundational because:\n- Darpan UID is mandatory for FCRA registration\n- it's required to be mentioned in your CSR-1 filing, and while general CSR-1 rules expect 3 years of track record, a newly formed trust set up specifically to carry out CSR activities is an exception\n- it's required for most government and corporate CSR funding channels, and the unique NITI Aayog ID signals verification and reliability to donors\n\nSince you already have a Darpan ID (per your message) \u2014 good, that's step one done.\n\n**2. Domain + hosting + CMS** \u2014 no eligibility gate at all\n- Your domain (dkbv.org) is registered \u2014 fine.\n- For CMS: WordPress (self-hosted or WordPress.com) is the most practical for a trust at your stage \u2014 free core software, huge plugin ecosystem for donations, volunteer forms, blog/updates, and it's what most Indian NGOs eventually migrate content into anyway. Alternatives: Wix/Squarespace (faster to launch, less flexible) or, once you're TechSoup-verified, Google Sites/Workspace-integrated options.\n- A relevant plugin stack for WordPress: **GiveWP** or **WPForms** (donation/volunteer forms), **Razorpay/Instamojo plugins** (payment gateway), **WP Mail SMTP** (receipts by email).\n\n**3. Payment gateway for donations** \u2014 you don't need 80G to *start* collecting donations, only to make them tax-deductible for the donor\nIf your NGO doesn't have a website yet, Instamojo lets you accept payments via a payment link or smart link even without one. Razorpay Payment Pages work similarly. The catch: 80G registration isn't mandatory to collect donations, but it's what lets donors claim tax deductions, which makes donations meaningfully more attractive to give. Practically:\n- You can start collecting via Razorpay/Instamojo now, issue plain donation receipts (name, PAN, amount, date, purpose) \u2014 just not 80G-tax-deduction receipts yet.\n- Once 80G comes through, Razorpay Payment Pages can auto-generate 80G receipts and email them to donors automatically, removing manual admin work, and Razorpay/Cashfree also offer a discounted nonprofit transaction rate (~1.5\u20131.75% vs standard ~2%) once you submit your 80G/12A certificate.\n\n**4. Meta (Facebook/Instagram) for the trust**\nTwo separate things, don't conflate them:\n- A Facebook Page + Instagram Business account, Meta Business Suite, ads, and the free **Meta Blueprint** marketing courses \u2014 all usable now, no registration gate.\n- Native in-platform fundraising tools (Donate button collecting money *inside* Facebook, Instagram donation stickers, PayPal Giving Fund payouts) \u2014 Meta's own fundraising tools that let donors give directly on Facebook/Instagram are only available in the US, UK, Canada and Australia; in other countries, Meta instead lets you use tools that drive people to donate on your own website. For India, the realistic path is: get your website's donation link reviewed and approved by Meta, after which shares of that link from Facebook/Instagram may surface a Donate button and related features. So \u2014 your own donation page (Razorpay/Instamojo-powered) is actually the more important asset than any Meta-native tool.\n\n**5. Google Ad Grants, Workspace, Microsoft, Canva, TechSoup** \u2014 pre-stage now, activate once 12A/80G lands\nStart the TechSoup India registration process now (it takes time on its own), so the moment 12A/80G certificates are issued you can submit them and unlock everything in one pass, rather than starting the vendor-verification clock only after tax registration.\n\n## Legal docs / receipts / trust management (your #4, #6, #7)\n\n- **Donation receipts now (pre-80G):** trust name, PAN, address, donor name, amount, date, mode of payment, purpose \u2014 legally fine, just not tax-deductible for the donor.\n- **Trust management basics you'll need regardless of 12A/80G status**: holding an AGM, maintaining a compliance manual for trustees, filing annual ITR even with no taxable income, and getting accounts audited once gross receipts cross exemption limits.\n- **When 12A/80G do land**: keep your Darpan details updated, and issue every donor a receipt carrying the trust's name, PAN, and 80G certificate number. Renewal of 12A/80G/FCRA is only needed if you actually hold them \u2014 a plain trust without these has a comparatively lighter compliance load, but that's also exactly why funders/CSR desks discount unregistered trusts, per Darpan-benefits research above.\n- **FCRA** \u2014 separate track, needs 3 years of existence before regular FCRA registration (or Prior Permission for a specific foreign donor in the interim), plus a dedicated SBI Delhi main branch FCRA account. Given your trust's 2019 registration date (even though dormant), it's worth getting a CA to check whether that counts toward the 3-year clock \u2014 that could be materially useful for FCRA timing.\n\n## Volunteers, CMS, everything-in-one-place\n\nFor a trust at your stage, don't over-engineer this yet \u2014 a lean stack:\n- **CMS**: WordPress site (public-facing: about, programs, donate, volunteer sign-up, updates/blog)\n- **Volunteer + donor data**: a simple Google Sheet or Zoho Forms/Airtable is genuinely fine at this scale; graduate to a proper CRM (Zoho for Nonprofits, or a donor-management tool) only once volunteer/donor numbers justify it\n- **Docs/records**: Google Workspace or Google Drive for trust deed, registration certificates, minutes, receipts \u2014 apply for the free nonprofit Workspace tier once TechSoup-verified\n\n## Suggested sequence\n\n1. Darpan \u2014 done (you have it)\n2. File 12A/80G (Form 10A) \u2014 you said this is already in motion for the next 1\u20132 months\n3. In parallel: launch the WordPress site on dkbv.org, connect Razorpay/Instamojo for donations, set up Facebook/Instagram pages\n4. Start TechSoup India registration now so verification is mid-process when 12A/80G certificates arrive\n5. Once 12A/80G land: submit to TechSoup \u2192 unlock Google Workspace, Canva, Microsoft 365 \u2192 apply for Google Ad Grants \u2192 switch Razorpay/Cashfree to nonprofit-rate + auto 80G receipts\n6. File Form CSR-1 (Darpan ID already covers that prerequisite) to become CSR-fund eligible\n7. At the 3-year mark (worth checking with a CA whether 2019 origin counts) \u2014 pursue FCRA if foreign donations are a goal\n\nA few of these tracks (TechSoup verification steps, exact Form 10A document checklist, CSR-1 filing specifics) go deeper than a quick search can responsibly cover \u2014 happy to dig into any one of them in more detail, or if useful, I can pull together a fuller multi-source compliance and grants roadmap specific to trusts in your state.",

                                "text": "Read also: Top 10 Fundraising Ideas for NGO in India \u00b7 Nonprofit organizations that qualify can receive up to $10,000 of in-kind search advertising per month through Google Ad Grants.\n\nIn today\u2019s digital era, digital marketing is crucial in growing nonprofits and raising funds to assist those in need.                                 "text": "Without 12A / 12AB, NGO income is taxable as ordinary income. Output: Registration under Sec 12AB. Validity: Provisional 3 years; final / renewed 5 years. (3) Sec 80G \u2014 Issuer: Income Tax Department. Purpose: Donor tax deduction (50% / 100% based on category) on donations to the NGO.\n\nThese four registrations form the core compliance stack for any NGO seeking serious institutional funding in India. (1) NGO Darpan \u2014 Issuer: NITI Aayog. Purpose: Central directory and verification. Output: Unique ID. Cost: Free. Validity: Lifetime with annual updates. (2) Sec 12A / 12AB \u2014 Issuer: Income Tax Department. Purpose: Income tax exemption for the NGO itself. Without 12A / 12AB, NGO income is taxable as ordinary income. Output: Registration under Sec 12AB. Validity: Provisional 3 years; final / renewed 5 years. (3) Sec 80G \u2014 Issuer: Income Tax Department. Purpose: Donor tax deduction (50% / 100% based on category) on donations to the NGO. \n\nWithout 80G, donors don't get tax benefit, donations dry up. Output: 80G approval certificate. Validity: Aligned with 12A. (4) Form CSR-1 \u2014 Issuer: Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA21 portal).                     {
                        "start_timestamp": "2026-08-03T14:24:19.961141Z",
                        "stop_timestamp": "2026-08-03T14:24:57.618860Z",
                        "flags": null,
                        "type": "text",
                        "text": "Good news first: even with 12A/80G/FCRA still pending, there's a lot you can set up *right now* that won't need to be redone later. Here's the landscape, mapped to your 8 requirements.\n\n## The core gating factor to understand\n\nAlmost every \"free tech/marketing for nonprofits\" program in India gates on **12A + 80G** (sometimes both, sometimes just one), verified through **TechSoup India**:\n\n- **Google Ad Grants** \u2014 up to $10,000/month in free Search ad credit \u2014 requires a registered charitable organization with a valid 12A/80G certificate and an active website with substantial content. Applied for via registering as an NGO member of NASSCOM Foundation's program, which acts as TechSoup's local partner in India for Google's verification.\n- **Google Workspace for Nonprofits** \u2014 available for NGOs registered under 12A and 80G, applied through TechSoup India, with 2\u20134 week processing.\n- **Microsoft 365 for Nonprofits** \u2014 Business Basic is free for up to 300 users for verified Indian nonprofits with 80G/12A status via Microsoft Philanthropies through TechSoup India, and Business Standard/Premium come at a steep discount.\n- **Canva for Nonprofits** \u2014 100% free Canva Pro access for registered NGOs, again gated behind TechSoup verification.\n- **TechSoup catalog broadly** (Adobe, Zoom, Slack for Charities, security tools) \u2014 same story: organizations must first be validated by TechSoup as operating on a nonprofit basis for the public benefit, then meet each vendor's individual criteria.\n\n**So the single highest-leverage move right now is pushing 12A/80G through fast** \u2014 everything downstream (Google, Microsoft, Canva, Ad Grants) unlocks off the back of that one certificate pair.\n\n## What you can do *this week*, with zero gating\n\n**1. NGO Darpan registration (do this immediately, it's free and has no minimum-age requirement)**\nA newly formed NGO can register on Darpan right away \u2014 there's no minimum age requirement, though applying for specific government schemes may require the org to be at least a year old. It's foundational because:\n- Darpan UID is mandatory for FCRA registration\n- it's required to be mentioned in your CSR-1 filing, and while general CSR-1 rules expect 3 years of track record, a newly formed trust set up specifically to carry out CSR activities is an exception\n- it's required for most government and corporate CSR funding channels, and the unique NITI Aayog ID signals verification and reliability to donors\n\nSince you already have a Darpan ID (per your message) \u2014 good, that's step one done.\n\n**2. Domain + hosting + CMS** \u2014 no eligibility gate at all\n- Your domain (dkbv.org) is registered \u2014 fine.\n- For CMS: WordPress (self-hosted or WordPress.com) is the most practical for a trust at your stage \u2014 free core software, huge plugin ecosystem for donations, volunteer forms, blog/updates, and it's what most Indian NGOs eventually migrate content into anyway. Alternatives: Wix/Squarespace (faster to launch, less flexible) or, once you're TechSoup-verified, Google Sites/Workspace-integrated options.\n- A relevant plugin stack for WordPress: **GiveWP** or **WPForms** (donation/volunteer forms), **Razorpay/Instamojo plugins** (payment gateway), **WP Mail SMTP** (receipts by email).\n\n**3. Payment gateway for donations** \u2014 you don't need 80G to *start* collecting donations, only to make them tax-deductible for the donor\nIf your NGO doesn't have a website yet, Instamojo lets you accept payments via a payment link or smart link even without one. Razorpay Payment Pages work similarly. The catch: 80G registration isn't mandatory to collect donations, but it's what lets donors claim tax deductions, which makes donations meaningfully more attractive to give. Practically:\n- You can start collecting via Razorpay/Instamojo now, issue plain donation receipts (name, PAN, amount, date, purpose) \u2014 just not 80G-tax-deduction receipts yet.\n- Once 80G comes through, Razorpay Payment Pages can auto-generate 80G receipts and email them to donors automatically, removing manual admin work, and Razorpay/Cashfree also offer a discounted nonprofit transaction rate (~1.5\u20131.75% vs standard ~2%) once you submit your 80G/12A certificate.\n\n**4. Meta (Facebook/Instagram) for the trust**\nTwo separate things, don't conflate them:\n- A Facebook Page + Instagram Business account, Meta Business Suite, ads, and the free **Meta Blueprint** marketing courses \u2014 all usable now, no registration gate.\n- Native in-platform fundraising tools (Donate button collecting money *inside* Facebook, Instagram donation stickers, PayPal Giving Fund payouts) \u2014 Meta's own fundraising tools that let donors give directly on Facebook/Instagram are only available in the US, UK, Canada and Australia; in other countries, Meta instead lets you use tools that drive people to donate on your own website. For India, the realistic path is: get your website's donation link reviewed and approved by Meta, after which shares of that link from Facebook/Instagram may surface a Donate button and related features. So \u2014 your own donation page (Razorpay/Instamojo-powered) is actually the more important asset than any Meta-native tool.\n\n**5. Google Ad Grants, Workspace, Microsoft, Canva, TechSoup** \u2014 pre-stage now, activate once 12A/80G lands\nStart the TechSoup India registration process now (it takes time on its own), so the moment 12A/80G certificates are issued you can submit them and unlock everything in one pass, rather than starting the vendor-verification clock only after tax registration.\n\n## Legal docs / receipts / trust management (your #4, #6, #7)\n\n- **Donation receipts now (pre-80G):** trust name, PAN, address, donor name, amount, date, mode of payment, purpose \u2014 legally fine, just not tax-deductible for the donor.\n- **Trust management basics you'll need regardless of 12A/80G status**: holding an AGM, maintaining a compliance manual for trustees, filing annual ITR even with no taxable income, and getting accounts audited once gross receipts cross exemption limits.\n- **When 12A/80G do land**: keep your Darpan details updated, and issue every donor a receipt carrying the trust's name, PAN, and 80G certificate number. Renewal of 12A/80G/FCRA is only needed if you actually hold them \u2014 a plain trust without these has a comparatively lighter compliance load, but that's also exactly why funders/CSR desks discount unregistered trusts, per Darpan-benefits research above.\n- **FCRA** \u2014 separate track, needs 3 years of existence before regular FCRA registration (or Prior Permission for a specific foreign donor in the interim), plus a dedicated SBI Delhi main branch FCRA account. Given your trust's 2019 registration date (even though dormant), it's worth getting a CA to check whether that counts toward the 3-year clock \u2014 that could be materially useful for FCRA timing.\n\n## Volunteers, CMS, everything-in-one-place\n\nFor a trust at your stage, don't over-engineer this yet \u2014 a lean stack:\n- **CMS**: WordPress site (public-facing: about, programs, donate, volunteer sign-up, updates/blog)\n- **Volunteer + donor data**: a simple Google Sheet or Zoho Forms/Airtable is genuinely fine at this scale; graduate to a proper CRM (Zoho for Nonprofits, or a donor-management tool) only once volunteer/donor numbers justify it\n- **Docs/records**: Google Workspace or Google Drive for trust deed, registration certificates, minutes, receipts \u2014 apply for the free nonprofit Workspace tier once TechSoup-verified\n\n## Suggested sequence\n\n1. Darpan \u2014 done (you have it)\n2. File 12A/80G (Form 10A) \u2014 you said this is already in motion for the next 1\u20132 months\n3. In parallel: launch the WordPress site on dkbv.org, connect Razorpay/Instamojo for donations, set up Facebook/Instagram pages\n4. Start TechSoup India registration now so verification is mid-process when 12A/80G certificates arrive\n5. Once 12A/80G land: submit to TechSoup \u2192 unlock Google Workspace, Canva, Microsoft 365 \u2192 apply for Google Ad Grants \u2192 switch Razorpay/Cashfree to nonprofit-rate + auto 80G receipts\n6. File Form CSR-1 (Darpan ID already covers that prerequisite) to become CSR-fund eligible\n7. At the 3-year mark (worth checking with a CA whether 2019 origin counts) \u2014 pursue FCRA if foreign donations are a goal\n\nA few of these tracks (TechSoup verification steps, exact Form 10A document checklist, CSR-1 filing specifics) go deeper than a quick search can responsibly cover \u2014 happy to dig into any one of them in more detail, or if useful, I can pull together a fuller multi-source compliance and grants roadmap specific to trusts in your state.",

                "text": "what I am saying is that I already have darpan id. And without 12A and 80G what benifits can I get, list only theme. Such as can I get the google one account or google workspace or anything similar digital assets in any means.",

            {
                "uuid": "019fc811-24f5-7d9e-af78-d615df45c7aa",
                "text": "I need to focus on extracting just the benefits and themes that don't require the 12A or 80G exemptions, particularly highlighting digital assets like Google accounts and workspace tools.Got it \u2014 here's what's realistically available to you **right now, without 12A/80G**, organized by theme:\n\n**1. Domain, hosting, CMS**\n- No gate at all. Your dkbv.org domain, WordPress/Wix/any CMS, hosting \u2014 fully usable now.\n\n**2. Payment collection for donations**\n- Razorpay/Instamojo payment links or a \"Donate\" page \u2014 usable now with just PAN + bank account + trust deed.\n- What you *don't* get yet: 80G tax-deduction receipts for donors, and the discounted nonprofit transaction rate (that needs 80G/12A on file).\n\n**3. Social media presence & marketing**\n- Facebook Page, Instagram Business account, Meta Business Suite, running paid ads, Meta Blueprint free courses \u2014 all usable now, no registration gate.\n- What you *don't* get: Meta's native in-platform fundraising/donate button (and that's actually India-restricted regardless \u2014 not available even with 12A/80G, only in US/UK/Canada/Australia).\n\n**4. Google ecosystem**\n- Regular Google Account/Gmail, a normal (paid) Google Workspace subscription \u2014 always available, no NGO status needed.\n- **Google Workspace for Nonprofits (free/discounted tier)** \u2014 gated, needs 12A + 80G + TechSoup verification.\n- **Google Ad Grants** ($10K/month ad credit) \u2014 gated, same requirement.\n- So: no free/discounted Google nonprofit product without 12A/80G. You can only use Google's *paid* consumer/business products in the meantime.\n\n**5. Microsoft, Canva, TechSoup catalog**\n- All gated behind 12A/80G + TechSoup verification. Nothing free here yet.\n- You *can* start the TechSoup India registration process now so it's mid-verification when your certificates land.\n\n**6. Government grants / CSR funding**\n- Not accessible yet without 12A/80G (and CSR-1 filing, which you can prep with your Darpan ID).\n\n**7. Volunteer & trust management tools**\n- Free-tier general tools (Google Forms, Zoho Forms, Airtable, spreadsheets) \u2014 no gate, usable now.\n\n**8. Credibility/legal**\n- Plain (non-80G) donation receipts \u2014 usable now.\n- FCRA \u2014 separate track, needs 3 years' existence regardless of 12A/80G timing.\n\n**Bottom line theme:** Darpan ID alone unlocks *nothing* directly discounted or free from Google/Microsoft/Meta \u2014 it mainly matters for FCRA eligibility and CSR-1 filing later. Everything branded \"for Nonprofits\" from big tech (Google, Microsoft, Canva) specifically needs 12A + 80G. Until then, you're operating on regular/paid consumer tools plus whatever's naturally free (social media, WordPress, forms, spreadsheets, payment links).",

                "uuid": "019fc81a-47a0-75b5-9268-a8f6d4963b1c",
                "text": "Good question. For a charitable trust site, you'd typically want maybe eight to ten pages: a home page, an about us page covering your mission and story, a page for your programs or causes, a donate page, a volunteer page, a page showing your trust registration and legal transparency documents, a gallery or impact stories page, a news or blog section, a contact page, and eventually a page for annual reports once you have them.\n\nNow, to actually build this out properly, here's what I'd need from you. First, the basics: what's the full name of the trust, and what's the story behind why you started it \u2014 like the actual mission statement in your own words. Second, what specific cause or causes are you focusing on \u2014 is it education, health, poverty relief, something else, or a mix? Third, do you have any photos, logos, or branding already, or are we starting from scratch there?\n\nFourth, who are the key people \u2014 trustees, founders \u2014 and do you want their names and photos on the site, or keep it more low-key for now? And fifth, do you already have any beneficiaries or activities you've done in these last twelve to fifteen days that we could showcase, even informally?\n\nWant to go through these one at a time, or do you want to just dump all the info you have and I'll organize it?",

                                "type": "text",
                                "text": "     1\t---\n     2\tname: pdf-reading\n     3\tdescription: \"Use this skill when you need to read, inspect, or extract content from PDF files \u2014 especially when file content is NOT in your context and you need to read it from disk. Covers content inventory, text extraction, page rasterization for visual inspection, embedded image/attachment/table/form-field extraction, and choosing the right reading strategy for different document types (text-heavy, scanned, slide-decks, forms, data-heavy). Do NOT use this skill for PDF creation, form filling, merging, splitting, watermarking, or encryption \u2014 use the pdf skill instead.\"\n     4\tlicense: Proprietary. LICENSE.txt has complete terms\n     5\t---\n     6\t\n     7\t# PDF Processing Guide\n     8\t\n     9\t## Overview\n    10\t\n    11\tThis guide covers essential PDF reading operations using Python libraries and command-line tools. For advanced features (pypdfium2 rendering, pdfplumber table settings, OCR fallback, encrypted/corrupted PDF handling), see REFERENCE.md.\n    12\t\n    13\t## Reading & Inspecting PDFs\n    14\t\n    15\tBefore doing anything with a PDF, understand what you're working with.\n    16\t\n    17\t### Content inventory\n    18\t\n    19\tRun a quick diagnostic first. For simple tasks (\"summarize this\n    20\tdocument\"), `pdfinfo` + `pdffonts` + a text sample may suffice. For\n    21\tanything involving figures, attachments, or extraction issues, run the\n    22\tfull set:\n    23\t\n    24\t```bash\n    25\t# Always: page count, file size, PDF version, metadata\n    26\tpdfinfo document.pdf\n    27\t\n    28\t# Always: does a text layer exist? No fonts \u2192 scanned/raster \u2192 see \"Scanned documents\"\n    29\tpdffonts document.pdf\n    30\t\n    31\t# If fonts are present: sample the text layer\n    32\tpdftotext -f 1 -l 1 document.pdf - | head -20\n    33\t\n    34\t# If figures/charts may matter:\n    35\tpdfimages -list document.pdf\n    36\t\n    37\t# If the PDF might contain embedded files (reports, portfolios):\n    38\tpdfdetach -list document.pdf\n    39\t```\n    40\t\n    41\tThis tells you:\n    42\t- **Page count and size** \u2014 how big is the job?\n    43\t- **Font status** \u2014 are any fonts present? An empty `pdffonts` table\n    44\t  means the PDF is scanned or raster-only: `pdftotext` will return\n    45\t  nothing, so skip straight to \"Scanned documents\" below. Fonts shown\n    46\t  as not embedded (\"emb: no\") with custom encodings may produce wrong\n    47\t  characters on extraction.\n    48\t- **Text extractability** \u2014 when fonts exist, does `pdftotext` return\n    49\t  clean text, or is it garbled (broken encoding)?\n    50\t- **Embedded raster images** \u2014 are there photos or raster figures?\n    51\t  (Note: vector-drawn charts from matplotlib/Excel won't appear \u2014 see\n    52\t  \"Extracting embedded images\" below)\n    53\t- **Attachments** \u2014 are there embedded spreadsheets, data files, etc.?\n    54\t\n    55\t### Text extraction\n    56\t\n    57\t**pypdf** for basic text:\n    58\t```python\n    59\tfrom pypdf import PdfReader\n    60\t\n    61\treader = PdfReader(\"document.pdf\")\n    62\tprint(f\"Pages: {len(reader.pages)}\")\n    63\t\n    64\t# Extract text\n    65\ttext = \"\"\n    66\tfor page in reader.pages:\n    67\t    text += page.extract_text()\n    68\t```\n    69\t\n    70\t**pdftotext** preserving layout (better for multi-column docs):\n    71\t```bash\n    72\t# Layout mode preserves spatial positioning\n    73\tpdftotext -layout document.pdf output.txt\n    74\t\n    75\t# Specific page range\n    76\tpdftotext -f 1 -l 5 document.pdf output.txt\n    77\t```\n    78\t\n    79\t**pdfplumber** for layout-aware extraction with positioning data:\n    80\t```python\n    81\timport pdfplumber\n    82\t\n    83\twith pdfplumber.open(\"document.pdf\") as pdf:\n    84\t    for page in pdf.pages:\n    85\t        text = page.extract_text()\n    86\t        print(text)\n    87\t```\n    88\t\n    89\t### Visual inspection (rasterize pages)\n    90\t\n    91\tText extraction is **blind** to charts, diagrams, figures, equations,\n    92\tmulti-column layout, and form structures. When any of these matter,\n    93\trasterize the relevant page and Read the image:\n    94\t\n    95\t```bash\n    96\t# Rasterize a single page (page 3 here) at 150 DPI\n    97\tpdftoppm -jpeg -r 150 -f 3 -l 3 document.pdf /tmp/page\n    98\t\n    99\t# pdftoppm zero-pads the output filename based on TOTAL page count\n   100\t# (e.g., page-03.jpg for a 50-page PDF, page-003.jpg for 200+ pages)\n   101\t# Don't guess the filename \u2014 find it:\n   102\tls /tmp/page-*.jpg\n   103\t```\n   104\t\n   105\tThen Read the resulting image file. This gives you full visual\n   106\tunderstanding of that page \u2014 layout, charts, equations, everything.\n   107\t\n   108\t**When to rasterize vs. text-extract:**\n   109\t- **Content/data questions \u2192 text extraction** (cheaper, searchable)\n   110\t- **Figures, charts, visual layout \u2192 rasterize the page**\n   111\t- **Tables \u2192 try text extraction first, rasterize if garbled**\n   112\t- **Precision matters \u2192 do both** (extract text AND rasterize; use text\n   113\t  for data, image for context \u2014 this is what Claude's API does natively\n   114\t  with PDF uploads)\n   115\t\n   116\t**Token cost awareness:**\n   117\t- Text extraction: ~200\u2013400 tokens per page\n   118\t- Rasterized image: ~1,600 tokens per page (at 150 DPI)\n   119\t- Both together: ~2,000\u20132,400 tokens per page\n   120\t\n   121\tFor a 100-page PDF, rasterizing everything would consume ~160K tokens.\n   122\tOnly rasterize pages that matter for the question at hand.\n   123\t\n   124\t### Choosing your reading strategy\n   125\t\n   126\t**Text-heavy documents** (reports, articles, books):\n   127\t\u2192 Text extraction is primary. Rasterize only for specific figures or\n   128\t  pages where layout matters.\n   129\t\n   130\t**Scanned documents** (`pdffonts` shows no fonts):\n   131\t\u2192 `pdftotext` will return nothing \u2014 don't run it. Rasterize pages at\n   132\t  150 DPI and Read them visually. For bulk text extraction, use OCR\n   133\t  (pytesseract after converting pages to images \u2014 see REFERENCE.md for\n   134\t  a complete example).\n   135\t\n   136\t**Slide-deck PDFs** (exported presentations):\n   137\t\u2192 Every page is primarily visual. Rasterize individual pages on demand.\n   138\t  Text extraction gives you bullet-point text but loses all layout.\n   139\t\n   140\t**Form-heavy documents**:\n   141\t\u2192 Extract form field values programmatically first (see below). Rasterize\n   142\t  the form page for visual context if needed.\n   143\t\n   144\t**Data-heavy documents** (tables, charts, figures):\n   145\t\u2192 Use pdfplumber for tables. Rasterize pages with charts/figures.\n   146\t  Extract text for surrounding narrative. Consider both text AND image\n   147\t  for the same page when precision matters.\n   148\t\n   149\t### Extracting embedded images\n   150\t\n   151\t```bash\n   152\t# List all embedded images with metadata (size, color, compression)\n   153\tpdfimages -list document.pdf\n   154\t\n   155\t# Extract all images as PNG\n   156\tpdfimages -png document.pdf /tmp/img\n   157\t\n   158\t# Extract from specific pages only (pages 3-5)\n   159\tpdfimages -png -f 3 -l 5 document.pdf /tmp/img\n   160\t\n   161\t# Extract in original format (JPEG stays JPEG, etc.)\n   162\tpdfimages -all document.pdf /tmp/img\n   163\t```\n   164\t\n   165\tThen Read `/tmp/img-000.png` (etc.) to see each extracted image.\n   166\t\n   167\t**Gotcha \u2014 vector graphics:** `pdfimages` extracts only raster image\n   168\tdata. Charts and diagrams drawn as vector graphics (common in\n   169\tmatplotlib, Excel, and R exports) will NOT appear \u2014 they are page\n   170\tcontent operators, not image objects. For these, rasterize the whole\n   171\tpage with `pdftoppm` instead.\n   172\t\n   173\t**Gotcha \u2014 empty images:** `pdfimages` sometimes produces many tiny or\n   174\tempty image files \u2014 these are typically background masks, transparency\n   175\tlayers, or decorative elements. Filter by file size to find the real\n   176\tcontent images.\n   177\t\n   178\tProgrammatic extraction with position data:\n   179\t```python\n   180\timport fitz  # PyMuPDF\n   181\t\n   182\tdoc = fitz.open(\"document.pdf\")\n   183\tfor page in doc:\n   184\t    for img in page.get_images():\n   185\t        xref = img[0]\n   186\t        pix = fitz.Pixmap(doc, xref)\n   187\t        if pix.n - pix.alpha > 3:  # CMYK or other non-RGB\n   188\t            pix = fitz.Pixmap(fitz.csRGB, pix)\n   189\t        pix.save(f\"/tmp/img_{xref}.png\")\n   190\t```\n   191\t\n   192\t### Extracting file attachments\n   193\t\n   194\tPDFs can contain embedded files \u2014 spreadsheets, data files, other\n   195\tdocuments. Common in business reports, PDF portfolios, and PDF/A-3\n   196\tcompliance documents.\n   197\t\n   198\t```bash\n   199\t# List all attachments\n   200\tpdfdetach -list document.pdf\n   201\t\n   202\t# Extract all attachments to a directory\n   203\tmkdir -p /tmp/attachments\n   204\tpdfdetach -saveall -o /tmp/attachments/ document.pdf\n   205\t\n   206\t# Extract a specific attachment by number (1-based index from -list output)\n   207\tpdfdetach -save 1 -o /tmp/attachment.pdf document.pdf\n   208\t```\n   209\t\n   210\tIn Python:\n   211\t```python\n   212\timport os\n   213\tfrom pypdf import PdfReader\n   214\t\n   215\treader = PdfReader(\"document.pdf\")\n   216\tfor name, content_list in reader.attachments.items():\n   217\t    safe_name = os.path.basename(name)  # sanitize \u2014 name comes from the PDF\n   218\t    for content in content_list:\n   219\t        with open(f\"/tmp/{safe_name}\", \"wb\") as f:\n   220\t            f.write(content)\n   221\t```\n   222\t\n   223\t**Two attachment mechanisms exist in PDFs:** page-level file annotation\n   224\tattachments (shown as paperclip icons in viewers) and document-level\n   225\tembedded files (in the EmbeddedFiles name tree). Both `pdfdetach` and\n   226\tpypdf handle the common cases. Rich media assets (3D, video) embedded\n   227\tas annotations may not appear in the attachment list \u2014 use PyMuPDF to\n   228\titerate page annotations for those.\n   229\t\n   230\t### Extracting form field data\n   231\t\n   232\tPDFs with interactive forms (government forms, applications, contracts)\n   233\thave fillable fields whose values can be read programmatically:\n   234\t\n   235\t```python\n   236\tfrom pypdf import PdfReader\n   237\t\n   238\treader = PdfReader(\"form.pdf\")\n   239\t\n   240\t# Text input fields only:\n   241\tfields = reader.get_form_text_fields()\n   242\tfor name, value in fields.items():\n   243\t    print(f\"{name}: {value}\")\n   244\t\n   245\t# All field types (checkboxes, radio buttons, dropdowns too):\n   246\tall_fields = reader.get_fields() or {}\n   247\tfor name, field in all_fields.items():\n   248\t    print(f\"{name}: {field.get('/V', '')} (type: {field.get('/FT', '')})\")\n   249\t```\n   250\t\n   251\t`get_form_text_fields()` returns only text input fields. For\n   252\tgovernment forms and contracts that use checkboxes, radio buttons,\n   253\tand dropdowns, use `get_fields()` instead to see all field types.\n   254\t\n   255\tFor comprehensive field info (types, options, defaults):\n   256\t```bash\n   257\tpdftk form.pdf dump_data_fields\n   258\t```\n   259\t\n   260\tFor anything beyond reading form data \u2014 filling forms, creating forms \u2014\n   261\tuse the `pdf` skill \u2014 invoke it by name if you have a Skill tool, or\n   262\tRead its SKILL.md (listed in your available skills, or in the same\n   263\tskills directory as this file).\n   264\t\n   265\t### Audio, video, and other rare embedded content\n   266\t\n   267\tPDFs can occasionally embed audio, video, or 3D models. Check\n   268\t`pdfdetach -list` first \u2014 if the media appears as an attachment,\n   269\textract with `pdfdetach -saveall`. If not, it may be a Rich Media\n   270\tannotation (harder to extract; requires PyMuPDF to iterate page\n   271\tannotations). This is very rare in practice. Most PDF viewers outside\n   272\tAdobe Acrobat do not support media playback.\n   273\t\n   274\t### Font diagnostics\n   275\t\n   276\tIf text extraction produces garbled output (wrong characters, missing\n   277\ttext, mojibake), look back at the `pdffonts` output from the Content\n   278\tinventory. Check the \"emb\" column \u2014 fonts showing \"no\" (not embedded)\n   279\twith custom encodings mean the PDF's character mapping may be broken\n   280\tfor text extraction. In that case, rasterize the page and use vision\n   281\tinstead.\n   282\t\n   283\tAlso check encoding: fonts with \"Custom\" or \"Identity-H\" encoding\n   284\twithout embedded CIDToGID maps can cause character substitution issues\n   285\teven when the font is technically embedded.\n   286\t\n   287\t---\n   288\t\n   289\t## Quick Reference\n   290\t\n   291\t| Task | Best Tool | Command/Code |\n   292\t|------|-----------|--------------|\n   293\t| Inspect PDF | poppler-utils | `pdfinfo`, `pdfimages -list`, `pdfdetach -list`, `pdffonts` |\n   294\t| Extract text | pdfplumber | `page.extract_text()` |\n   295\t| Extract text (CLI) | pdftotext | `pdftotext -layout input.pdf output.txt` |\n   296\t| Extract tables | pdfplumber | `page.extract_tables()` |\n   297\t| See page visually | pdftoppm | `pdftoppm -jpeg -r 150 -f N -l N` |\n   298\t| Extract images | pdfimages | `pdfimages -png input.pdf prefix` |\n   299\t| Extract attachments | pdfdetach | `pdfdetach -saveall -o /tmp/` |\n   300\t| Read form fields | pypdf | `reader.get_fields()` |\n   301\t| OCR scanned PDFs | pytesseract | Convert to image first |\n   302\t\n   303\t## PDF Form Filling, Creation, Merging, Splitting, and Other Operations\n   304\t\n   305\tThis skill covers **reading and inspection** only. For filling forms,\n   306\tcreating, merging, splitting, rotating, watermarking, encrypting, or\n   307\tother PDF manipulation tasks, use the `pdf` skill (find its SKILL.md\n   308\tlocation in your available skills).\n   309\t",
                                "uuid": "a58bb062-b165-4a77-b4f5-24f78734aad1"
                            }
                        ],

                    {
                        "start_timestamp": "2026-08-03T14:53:20.046239Z",
                        "stop_timestamp": "2026-08-03T14:53:20.046257Z",
                        "flags": null,

                        "name": "view",
                        "content": [
                            {
                                "type": "text",
                                "text": "     1\t---\n     2\tname: docx\n     3\tdescription: \"Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx files) or Word templates (.dotx files). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, headings, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, performing find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file, use this skill. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or general coding tasks unrelated to document generation.\"\n     4\tlicense: Proprietary. LICENSE.txt has complete terms\n     5\t---\n     6\t\n     7\t# DOCX creation, editing, and analysis\n     8\t\n     9\tA `.docx` is a ZIP archive of XML files. Choose your approach by task:\n    10\t\n    11\t| Task | Approach |\n    12\t|---|---|\n    13\t| **Create** a new document | Write a `docx` (npm) script \u2014 see gotchas below |\n    14\t| **Edit** an existing document | `unzip` \u2192 edit `word/document.xml` \u2192 `zip` (docx-js cannot open existing files) |\n    15\t| **Read** content | `pandoc -t markdown file.docx` |\n    16\t\n    17\t> Script paths below are relative to this skill's directory.\n    18\t\n    19\t## Creating with docx-js \u2014 gotchas\n    20\t\n    21\t`docx` is preinstalled \u2014 do not run `npm install` first; write the script and `require('docx')` directly. Only if that require fails: `npm install docx`. The model knows the API; these are the footguns:\n    22\t\n    23\t- **Page size defaults to A4.** For US Letter set `page: { size: { width: 12240, height: 15840 } }` (DXA; 1440 = 1\u2033).\n    24\t- **Landscape:** pass portrait dimensions and `orientation: PageOrientation.LANDSCAPE` \u2014 docx-js swaps width/height internally.\n    25\t- **Tables need dual widths:** set `columnWidths` on the table AND `width` on every cell, both in `WidthType.DXA` (PERCENTAGE breaks in Google Docs). Column widths must sum to the table width.\n    26\t- **Table shading:** use `ShadingType.CLEAR`, never `SOLID` (renders black).\n    27\t- **Lists:** never insert `\u2022` literally; use a `numbering` config with `LevelFormat.BULLET`.\n    28\t- **`ImageRun` requires `type:`** (`\"png\"`, `\"jpg\"`, \u2026).\n    29\t- **`PageBreak` must be inside a `Paragraph`.**\n    30\t- **Never use `\\n`** \u2014 use separate `Paragraph` elements.\n    31\t- **TOC:** headings must use built-in `HeadingLevel.*`; custom heading styles need `outlineLevel` set or they won't appear.\n    32\t- **Don't use a table as a horizontal rule** \u2014 use a paragraph bottom border instead.\n    33\t- **Dot-leader / right-aligned-on-same-line:** use `PositionalTab` (`alignment: PositionalTabAlignment.RIGHT`, `leader: PositionalTabLeader.DOT`) inside a `TextRun`, not literal `.` or space padding.\n    34\t\n    35\t## Verify the output\n    36\t\n    37\tAfter writing a `.docx`, render it and look at it:\n    38\t\n    39\t```bash\n    40\tpython scripts/office/soffice.py --headless --convert-to pdf output.docx\n    41\tpdftoppm -jpeg -r 100 output.pdf page\n    42\tls page-*.jpg   # then Read the images\n    43\t```\n    44\t\n    45\t`pdftoppm` zero-pads page numbers to the width of the page count (`page-01.jpg`\u2026`page-12.jpg`).\n    46\t\n    47\t## Editing existing documents\n    48\t\n    49\tLegacy `.doc` files must be converted first: `python scripts/office/soffice.py --headless --convert-to docx file.doc`.\n    50\t\n    51\t```bash\n    52\tunzip -q doc.docx -d unpacked/\n    53\tfind unpacked -type l -delete   # strip symlink entries \u2014 docx from external parties is untrusted\n    54\tpython scripts/merge_runs.py unpacked/   # coalesce fragmented runs so text is findable\n    55\t# edit unpacked/word/document.xml in place \u2014 do NOT reformat or pretty-print\n    56\t(cd unpacked && rm -f ../out.docx && zip -Xr ../out.docx .)\n    57\tpython scripts/office/validate.py out.docx --original doc.docx   # XSD checks; --auto-repair fixes common issues\n    58\t# redlining? add --author \"<the name you redlined under>\" to check every edit is tracked\n    59\t```\n    60\t\n    61\tWord splits text across many `<w:r>` runs (revision ids, spell-check markers), so a phrase you can see in the document often doesn't exist as a contiguous string in the XML. `merge_runs.py` merges adjacent identically-formatted runs in `word/document.xml` without changing content or rendering; it also accepts a `.docx` directly (`python scripts/merge_runs.py doc.docx -o merged.docx`).\n    62\t\n    63\t**Tracked changes:** when redlining, validate with `--author \"<the name you redlined under>\"` (needs `--original`) \u2014 it reports any text you changed without a `<w:ins>`/`<w:del>` around it, which is easy to do by accident and invisible in the accepted view. Wrap runs in `<w:ins>`/`<w:del>` with `w:id`, `w:author`, `w:date` attributes. Inside `<w:del>`, the text element is `<w:delText>`, not `<w:t>`. A deleted paragraph mark (`<w:pPr><w:rPr><w:del w:id=\"..\" w:author=\"..\" w:date=\"..\"/></w:rPr></w:pPr>`) means \"merge this paragraph into the next\" \u2014 so deleting a paragraph outright is that plus a `<w:del>` around every run. The `<w:del/>` must come before the rPr's other children; their order is schema-enforced.\n    64\t\n    65\tTo produce a clean copy with all tracked changes accepted: `python scripts/accept_changes.py in.docx out.docx`.\n    66\t\n    67\tAccepting a deleted paragraph mark should join that paragraph to the one below it, so a paragraph whose runs are *all* deleted vanishes. Word does this; `accept_changes.py` and `pandoc --track-changes=accept` don't always. Both fail the same way \u2014 they strip the deleted text but leave the emptied paragraph behind, which reads as a stray empty bullet when it was auto-numbered:\n    68\t\n    69\t- `pandoc --track-changes=accept` never joins the paragraphs.\n    70\t- `accept_changes.py` (LibreOffice) joins them correctly, except when the deleted paragraph is followed by an empty spacer paragraph.\n    71\t\n    72\tAn empty bullet in either view is an artifact of that view, not a defect in the document. Check paragraph deletions in the XML.\n    73\t\n    74\t## Comments\n    75\t\n    76\tComments require six cross-linked files. Use the helper \u2014 directory mode when you'll also be editing `document.xml` (saves an unzip/rezip cycle), `.docx`-direct mode otherwise:\n    77\t\n    78\t```bash\n    79\t# Against an already-unpacked directory (preferred when also placing markers)\n    80\tpython scripts/comment.py unpacked/ \"Fees & expenses cap is too low\"\n    81\tpython scripts/comment.py unpacked/ \"Agreed\" --parent 0\n    82\t\n    83\t# Against a .docx directly\n    84\tpython scripts/comment.py contract.docx \"This cap is too low\" -o annotated.docx\n    85\t```\n    86\t\n    87\tThe script writes `comments.xml`, `commentsExtended.xml`, `commentsIds.xml`, `commentsExtensible.xml`, the relationships, and the content-type overrides. Comment IDs are auto-assigned. It then prints the `<w:commentRangeStart>`/`<w:commentRangeEnd>`/`<w:commentReference>` snippet to add to `word/document.xml` so the comment anchors to specific text \u2014 until you place those markers, the comment exists but is not visible.\n    88\t\n    89\t## Dependencies\n    90\t\n    91\t`docx` (npm, preinstalled \u2014 install only if `require('docx')` fails) \u00b7 `pandoc` \u00b7 LibreOffice (`soffice`) \u00b7 `pdftoppm` (Poppler)\n    92\t",
                                "uuid": "da7f815d-6140-48b6-8d7a-c978230a66b9"
                            }
                        ],

                            "file_text": "const {\n  Document, Packer, Paragraph, TextRun, HeadingLevel, Table, TableRow, TableCell,\n  WidthType, ShadingType, BorderStyle, AlignmentType, PageBreak, VerticalAlign\n} = require(\"docx\");\n\nconst TABLE_WIDTH = 9350; // DXA, fits within margins on A4\nconst COL_WIDTHS = [3100, 3400, 2850]; // Question | What we found | Your input\n\nfunction headerRow() {\n  return new TableRow({\n    tableHeader: true,\n    children: [\"Question\", \"What we found in your uploaded documents\", \"Your answer / confirmation\"].map((text, i) =>\n      new TableCell({\n        width: { size: COL_WIDTHS[i], type: WidthType.DXA },\n        shading: { type: ShadingType.CLEAR, color: \"auto\", fill: \"2F5233\" },\n        verticalAlign: VerticalAlign.CENTER,\n        children: [new Paragraph({\n          children: [new TextRun({ text, bold: true, color: \"FFFFFF\", size: 20 })]\n        })]\n      })\n    )\n  });\n}\n\nfunction row(question, found, blankNote) {\n  return new TableRow({\n    children: [\n      new TableCell({\n        width: { size: COL_WIDTHS[0], type: WidthType.DXA },\n        children: [new Paragraph({ children: [new TextRun({ text: question, size: 20 })] })]\n      }),\n      new TableCell({\n        width: { size: COL_WIDTHS[1], type: WidthType.DXA },\n        children: [new Paragraph({ children: [new TextRun({ text: found, size: 20, italics: !found.startsWith(\"Found\") ? false : false })] })]\n      }),\n      new TableCell({\n        width: { size: COL_WIDTHS[2], type: WidthType.DXA },\n        children: [new Paragraph({ children: [new TextRun({ text: blankNote || \"\", size: 20, color: \"808080\", italics: true })] })]\n      })\n    ]\n  });\n}\n\nfunction sectionHeading(num, title) {\n  return new Paragraph({\n    heading: HeadingLevel.HEADING_1,\n    spacing: { before: 400, after: 150 },\n    children: [new TextRun({ text: `${num}. ${title}` })]\n  });\n}\n\nfunction sectionTable(rows) {\n  return new Table({\n    width: { size: TABLE_WIDTH, type: WidthType.DXA },\n    columnWidths: COL_WIDTHS,\n    rows: [headerRow(), ...rows]\n  });\n}\n\nfunction note(text) {\n  return new Paragraph({\n    spacing: { before: 100, after: 200 },\n    children: [new TextRun({ text, italics: true, size: 18, color: \"555555\" })]\n  });\n}\n\nconst doc = new Document({\n  sections: [{\n    properties: {\n      page: { size: { width: 11906, height: 16838 } } // A4\n    },\n    children: [\n      new Paragraph({\n        heading: HeadingLevel.TITLE,\n        children: [new TextRun({ text: \"DKBV Charitable Trust \u2014 Website Blueprint Questionnaire\" })]\n      }),\n      new Paragraph({\n        spacing: { before: 100, after: 100 },\n        children: [new TextRun({ text: \"Prepared to plan the complete structure and content of dkbv.org\", italics: true, size: 22 })]\n      }),\n      new Paragraph({\n        spacing: { after: 300 },\n        children: [new TextRun({\n          text: \"I reviewed all the documents currently in your project (Trust Deed, Trust Registration Form, Anusuchi/Schedule, Vakilatnama, Sogandnama/Affidavit, Sammati Patra, PAN card, and the affidavit draft). Several of these are handwritten or stamp-paper scans in Gujarati, so wherever a detail could not be read with full confidence, that is marked clearly below instead of being guessed. Please fill in the third column (directly in this file, or by replying to me) and I'll turn it into the full website blueprint.\",\n          size: 20\n        })]\n      }),\n\n      // 1. Legal identity\n      sectionHeading(1, \"Trust Identity & Legal Basics\"),\n      sectionTable([\n        row(\"Full legal name of the trust exactly as in the Trust Deed\",\n          \"Your domain is dkbv.org \u2014 please confirm the exact full legal name (in English and, if relevant, in Gujarati) as it appears on the Trust Deed, so the website header/footer/legal pages match your registered name exactly.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Trust PAN\",\n          \"Found: PAN AAZTS5070P appears associated with your Trust documents (from the affidavit file naming). Please confirm this is the Trust's PAN (not an individual's).\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Date & place of original registration\",\n          \"You mentioned the trust was originally registered in 2019 and was inactive until you restarted it 12\u201315 days ago. The Trust Deed and Registration Form are in Gujarati/on stamp paper \u2014 please confirm the exact registration date, registration number, and the Registrar/Sub-Registrar office and district (documents suggest Gujarat).\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Registered office address\",\n          \"Not confidently readable from the scanned deed/registration form. Please provide the full registered address exactly as it should appear on the website footer and legal/transparency page.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"NGO Darpan ID / Unique ID\",\n          \"You've confirmed you already have this. Please share the ID itself so it can be listed on the transparency page.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Current status: 12A / 80G / FCRA\",\n          \"Per our earlier discussion: 12A and 80G are in process (expected within 1\u20132 months); FCRA not yet applicable. The site should reflect current status honestly and be easy to update once certificates arrive.\",\n          \"\"),\n      ]),\n      note(\"Why this matters: the trust's legal name, PAN, registration number and address are what typically go on the footer, the \\\"Legal & Transparency\\\" page, and donation receipts \u2014 getting these exact avoids reprinting/re-issuing anything later.\"),\n\n      // 2. Story / mission\n      sectionHeading(2, \"Story, Mission & Vision\"),\n      sectionTable([\n        row(\"Why was this trust started? (founder's own words)\",\n          \"Not in the documents \u2014 this is personal/narrative content only you can provide.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Mission statement (1\u20132 sentences)\",\n          \"The Trust Deed will contain formal \\\"objects of the trust\\\" clauses in legal language \u2014 I can pull the closest matching language from the deed once you confirm the exact wording is correct, but a shorter, warmer version for the homepage needs to come from you.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Long-term vision (3\u20135 years out)\",\n          \"Not in the documents.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Tagline / one-line description for the homepage banner\",\n          \"Not in the documents.\",\n          \"\"),\n      ]),\n\n      // 3. Programs and causes\n      sectionHeading(3, \"Programs & Focus Areas\"),\n      sectionTable([\n        row(\"Official \\\"objects\\\" / permitted charitable activities of the trust\",\n          \"The Trust Deed contains a formal objects clause (typically education, health, poverty relief, relief of distress, or similar, depending on how it was drafted in 2019). This needs a careful re-read against the original document with you to transcribe correctly \u2014 legal wording matters here since it should match what's registered.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Which of those objects are you actually planning to actively work on right now?\",\n          \"Not in the documents \u2014 depends on your current plans.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Specific programs/causes for the website (e.g. education, healthcare, disaster relief, elderly care)\",\n          \"Not in the documents.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Any geographic focus (city/district/state, or pan-India)?\",\n          \"Documents suggest a Gujarat registration; please confirm if activities are geographically focused or open nationally.\",\n          \"\"),\n      ]),\n\n      // 4. People\n      sectionHeading(4, \"Trustees & People\"),\n      sectionTable([\n        row(\"Names of all trustees / founding members\",\n          \"The Trust Deed and Vakilatnama (power of attorney) will list trustee names and signatures, but names on scanned stamp-paper documents in Gujarati could not be transcribed with full confidence. Please provide the list of trustee names exactly as spelled, along with designations (Chairman/President/Secretary/Treasurer/Trustee).\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Do you want trustee names & photos publicly listed on the site?\",\n          \"Not in the documents \u2014 your call. Many donors/CSR desks expect at least names + designations to be public for credibility.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Any staff or core team beyond trustees?\",\n          \"Not applicable yet, given the trust restarted 12\u201315 days ago \u2014 but worth listing whoever is actively involved.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"PAN card holder shown in Aadarsh_card_Pancard.pdf \u2014 is this the Trust's PAN or an individual trustee's personal PAN?\",\n          \"The uploaded PAN card image needs confirmation of whose PAN this is and in what capacity, so it isn't misrepresented on the site or receipts.\",\n          \"\"),\n      ]),\n\n      // 5. Branding\n      sectionHeading(5, \"Branding & Visual Identity\"),\n      sectionTable([\n        row(\"Do you have a logo already?\",\n          \"Not found in the documents.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Preferred colors / visual style (e.g. warm and community-driven, formal and institutional, modern and minimal)\",\n          \"Not in the documents.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Any existing photos (of activities, beneficiaries, trustees) to use?\",\n          \"Not in the documents.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Do you have any activities from the last 12\u201315 days that could be photographed/documented for the site?\",\n          \"Not in the documents \u2014 even informal photos help credibility for a brand-new site.\",\n          \"\"),\n      ]),\n\n      // 6. Donations\n      sectionHeading(6, \"Donations & Payments\"),\n      sectionTable([\n        row(\"Bank account details for donations (account name, must match trust's legal name)\",\n          \"Not extracted from documents \u2014 please confirm bank name, account number, IFSC, and that the account is in the trust's name (not a personal account).\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Preferred payment gateway (Razorpay / Instamojo / Cashfree / other)\",\n          \"Not decided yet, per our earlier discussion. Needs PAN, bank passbook/cheque, trust deed to activate.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Minimum donation amount / suggested donation tiers?\",\n          \"Not in the documents \u2014 your preference.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Should donations be one-time only, or also recurring/monthly?\",\n          \"Not in the documents \u2014 your preference.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Receipt format details (until 80G arrives): what should the plain receipt show?\",\n          \"Standard fields: trust name, PAN, address, donor name, amount, date, mode of payment, purpose. Confirm if anything else should be added.\",\n          \"\"),\n      ]),\n\n      // 7. Volunteers\n      sectionHeading(7, \"Volunteers\"),\n      sectionTable([\n        row(\"What kind of volunteer roles are you looking for right now?\",\n          \"Not in the documents.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Do you want an online volunteer sign-up form, or just a contact-us based process for now?\",\n          \"Not in the documents.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Any minimum commitment, age requirement, or location constraint for volunteers?\",\n          \"Not in the documents.\",\n          \"\"),\n      ]),\n\n      // 8. Legal/transparency\n      sectionHeading(8, \"Legal & Transparency Page\"),\n      sectionTable([\n        row(\"Should the registration certificate, PAN, and (once available) 12A/80G certificates be uploaded as viewable/downloadable documents on the site?\",\n          \"Not in the documents \u2014 many donor-facing NGO sites do this for credibility; your call on visibility.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Darpan ID display\",\n          \"You have the ID; please share it for display once you're ready.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Privacy policy / refund-cancellation policy for donations\",\n          \"Not in the documents \u2014 standard boilerplate can be drafted once payment gateway is chosen.\",\n          \"\"),\n      ]),\n\n      // 9. Contact & social\n      sectionHeading(9, \"Contact & Social Presence\"),\n      sectionTable([\n        row(\"Contact email(s) \u2014 general, donations, volunteering\",\n          \"Not in the documents.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Phone number(s) to display\",\n          \"Not in the documents.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Social media handles (Facebook, Instagram, etc.) \u2014 existing or to be created?\",\n          \"Not set up yet, per our earlier discussion \u2014 to be created alongside the website.\",\n          \"\"),\n        row(\"Physical address to display (may differ from registered address)\",\n          \"Not in the documents.\",\n          \"\"),\n      ]),\n\n      // 10. Site structure recap\n      new Paragraph({ children: [new PageBreak()] }),\n      sectionHeading(10, \"Proposed Website Structure (for your review)\"),\n      new Paragraph({\n        spacing: { after: 150 },\n        children: [new TextRun({ text: \"Based on what a charitable trust site typically needs, here's a working page list \u2014 tell me what to add, remove, or merge:\", size: 20 })]\n      }),\n      ...[\n        \"Home \u2014 mission snapshot, tagline, hero image/photo, quick links to Donate/Volunteer\",\n        \"About Us \u2014 story, mission, vision, trust registration basics\",\n        \"Our Programs / Causes \u2014 one section per focus area\",\n        \"Donate \u2014 payment options, bank details, what donations fund\",\n        \"Volunteer \u2014 roles, sign-up form or contact process\",\n        \"Legal & Transparency \u2014 registration certificate, PAN, Darpan ID, 12A/80G once available\",\n        \"Gallery / Impact Stories \u2014 photos and updates from activities\",\n        \"News / Updates (blog) \u2014 ongoing activity log, useful for credibility and for Google indexing later\",\n        \"Contact Us \u2014 email, phone, address, social links\",\n        \"Annual Reports (added later once you have a full year of activity/financials)\"\n      ].map(t => new Paragraph({\n        bullet: { level: 0 },\n        spacing: { after: 80 },\n        children: [new TextRun({ text: t, size: 20 })]\n      })),\n\n      new Paragraph({\n        spacing: { before: 300 },\n        children: [new TextRun({\n          text: \"Once you fill in the columns above (or just reply with the answers), I'll turn this into the complete website blueprint \u2014 page-by-page content, structure, and what goes live now vs. what gets added once 12A/80G/FCRA come through.\",\n          italics: true, size: 20\n        })]\n      })\n    ]\n  }]\n});\n\nPacker.toBuffer(doc).then(buf => require(\"fs\").writeFileSync(\"/home/claude/website_blueprint/DKBV_Website_Blueprint_Questionnaire.docx\", buf));\n"
                        },
